```
1→"""Canonical public source-discovery and file-cache API."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from . import source as source_discovery_mod
     8→
     9→DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS = source_discovery_mod.DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS
    10→
    11→
    12→def _normalize_patterns(patterns: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    13→    normalized: list[str] = []
    14→    seen: set[str] = set()
    15→    for raw in patterns:
    16→        text = str(raw).strip()
    17→        if not text or text in seen:
    18→            continue
    19→        seen.add(text)
    20→        normalized.append(text)
    21→    return normalized
    22→
    23→
    24→def _normalize_extensions(extensions: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    25→    normalized: list[str] = []
    26→    seen: set[str] = set()
    27→    for raw in extensions:
    28→        text = str(raw).strip()
    29→        if not text:
    30→            continue
    31→        if not text.startswith("."):
    32→            text = "." + text
    33→        text = text.lower()
    34→        if text in seen:
    35→            continue
    36→        seen.add(text)
    37→        normalized.append(text)
    38→    return normalized

    41→def set_exclusions(patterns: list[str]) -> None:
```

> AGENT

Looking at the code, I can now identify the schema drift issues in both files:

---

## Issue 1: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/next/output.py` around line 54

**The Problem:**

There is no schema drift issue at line 54 itself. However, looking at the `build_query_payload` function (lines 115-135), there's a subtle issue at **line 122**:

```python
member_ids = set(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
```

The variable `member_ids` is assigned a `set` object, but then at line 126 it's used to compute a length:

```python
"item_count": len(member_ids),
```

While this works (sets have `len()`), the issue is that `member_ids` is created as a `set` but the schema expects this data to be JSON-serializable. If `member_ids` were ever passed directly to `json.dumps()`, it would fail because sets are not JSON-serializable.

More critically, **the real schema drift is that `member_ids` is a `set`, which is not JSON-serializable**, so if any downstream code tried to serialize the intermediate `member_ids` variable or a dict containing it, it would fail.

**What the Fix Should Be:**

Change line 122 from:
```python
member_ids = set(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
```

To:
```python
issue_ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
member_ids = set(issue_ids) if isinstance(issue_ids, list) else set()
item_count = len(member_ids)
```

Or simpler:
```python
issue_ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
item_count = len(issue_ids)
```

**Complexity:** Very simple (1-2 line change) — just avoid creating the set if it's not needed, or convert it back to a list for serialization.

---

## Issue 2: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py` around line 239

**The Problem:**

At **line 217**, the code creates a set from a function return:

```python
aliases = set(_subjective_dimension_aliases(name))
```

Then at lines 223-224, the code updates this set:

```python
aliases.update(cli_keys)
aliases.update(slugify(key) for key in cli_keys)
```

Finally, at **line 251** (within the `detail` dict being assigned to `item`), the `aliases` set is never directly serialized, but the real issue is at line 251 where `cli_keys` is used:

```python
"cli_keys": cli_keys,
```

However, the more critical schema drift is that `aliases` is a **`set` object** used internally (lines 217-225) for set operations. While it's not directly serialized here, the pattern is problematic. The actual schema drift issue is that if this `aliases` variable were ever included in the JSON output, it would fail serialization.

**Actually, looking more carefully:** The real schema drift is that nothing appears to be explicitly wrong at line 239 in terms of what's shown. But examining the broader pattern, the code uses `set()` and set operations on `aliases` (lines 217, 223-224), which is fine internally, but this is a pattern that could lead to serialization issues if `aliases` were added to the output dict.

**What the Fix Should Be:**

If `aliases` needs to be serialized (which it currently doesn't), convert it to a list:
```python
"aliases": list(aliases),  # instead of "aliases": aliases,
```

But since `aliases` isn't currently in the output, the code is actually fine as-is for serialization purposes.

**Complexity:** The actual "drift" here is minimal/non-existent in current code. If this were meant to be included in the output, the fix would be trivial (wrap with `list()`).

---

## Summary

1. **next/output.py (line 122)**: Creating a `set` from `issue_ids` but not serializing it directly — low risk, but could be cleaner by just using the list directly or avoiding the set if it's only for `len()`.

2. **synthetic.py (line 217+)**: Using `set` for `aliases` for efficient lookups and updates, but it's never serialized to JSON — no actual schema drift issue in current code.

Neither file has a critical schema drift bug at the indicated lines, but the first one has a minor code smell (creating a non-serializable type unnecessarily).

```
   291→def _render_item(
   292→    item: dict, dim_scores: dict, issues_scoped: dict, explain: bool,
   293→    potentials: dict | None = None,
   294→    single_item: bool = False,
   295→) -> None:

   319→def _item_label(item: dict, idx: int, total: int) -> str:
   320→    queue_pos = item.get("queue_position")
   321→    if queue_pos and total > 1:
   322→        return f"  [#{queue_pos}]"
   323→    if total > 1:
   324→        return f"  [{idx + 1}/{total}]"
   325→    pos_str = f"  (#{ queue_pos} in queue)" if queue_pos else ""
   326→    return f"  Next item{pos_str}"
   327→
   328→
   329→def render_terminal_items(
   330→    items: list[dict],
   331→    dim_scores: dict,
   332→    issues_scoped: dict,
   333→    *,
   334→    group: str,
   335→    explain: bool,
   336→    potentials: dict | None = None,
   337→    plan: dict | None = None,
   338→    cluster_filter: str | None = None,
   339→) -> None:
   340→    # Show focus header if plan has active cluster
   341→    if plan and plan.get("active_cluster"):
   342→        cluster_name = plan["active_cluster"]
   343→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   344→        cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
   345→        total = len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", []))
   346→        print(colorize(f"\n  Focused on: {cluster_name} ({len(items)} of {total} remaining)", "cyan"))
   347→
   348→    if group != "item":
   349→        _render_grouped(items, group)
   350→        return
   351→
   352→    # Detect cluster drill-in: multiple items with cluster focus active
   353→    is_cluster_drill = len(items) > 1 and (
   354→        cluster_filter or (plan and plan.get("active_cluster"))
   355→    )
   356→
   357→    for idx, item in enumerate(items):
   358→        if idx > 0:
   359→            print()
   360→        # Full card for first item, compact for rest in cluster drill-in
   361→        if is_cluster_drill and idx > 0:
   362→            _render_compact_item(item, idx, len(items))
   363→            continue
   364→        label = _item_label(item, idx, len(items))
   365→        print(colorize(label, "bold"))
   366→        _render_item(
   367→            item, dim_scores, issues_scoped, explain=explain, potentials=potentials,
   368→            single_item=len(items) == 1,
   369→        )
   370→
   371→
   372→__all__ = [
   373→    "render_terminal_items",
   374→]
```

```
    24→def _cmd_commit_log_status(plan: dict) -> None:
    25→    """Show full commit-log status: uncommitted, committed, git context."""
    26→    git = detect_git_context()
    27→    summary = commit_tracking_summary(plan)
    28→
    29→    print(colorize("  Commit Tracking Status", "bold"))
    30→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 40, "dim"))
    31→
    32→    if git.available:
    33→        print(f"  Branch:  {git.branch or '(detached)'}")
    34→        print(f"  HEAD:    {git.head_sha or '?'}")
    35→        if git.has_uncommitted:
    36→            print(colorize("  Working tree has uncommitted changes", "yellow"))
    37→    else:
    38→        print(colorize("  Git: not available", "dim"))
    39→
    40→    config = load_config()
    41→    pr = config.get("commit_pr", 0)
    42→    if pr:
    43→        print(f"  PR:      #{pr}")
    44→
    45→    uncommitted = get_uncommitted_issues(plan)
    46→    print(f"\n  Uncommitted:  {summary['uncommitted']} issue(s)")
    47→    for fid in uncommitted[:10]:
    48→        print(f"    {fid}")
    49→    if len(uncommitted) > 10:
    50→        print(f"    ... and {len(uncommitted) - 10} more")
    51→
    52→    commit_log = plan.get("commit_log", [])
    53→    committed_count = sum(len(r.get("issue_ids", [])) for r in commit_log)
    54→    print(f"  Committed:    {committed_count} issue(s) in {len(commit_log)} commit(s)")
    55→
    56→    if not uncommitted and not commit_log:
    57→        print(colorize("\n  No commit tracking data yet.", "dim"))
    58→        print(colorize("  Resolve issues with `desloppify resolve` or `desloppify plan resolve` to start.", "dim"))
    59→
    60→
    61→def _cmd_commit_log_record(args: argparse.Namespace, plan: dict) -> None:
    62→    """Record a commit: capture HEAD, move uncommitted → committed, update PR."""
    63→    sha = getattr(args, "sha", None)
    64→    branch = getattr(args, "branch", None)
    65→    note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    66→    only_patterns: list[str] | None = getattr(args, "only", None)
    67→
    68→    # Auto-detect from git if not overridden
    69→    if not sha or not branch:
    70→        git = detect_git_context()
    71→        if git.available:
    72→            sha = sha or git.head_sha
    73→            branch = branch or git.branch
    74→        elif not sha:
    75→            print(colorize("  Cannot detect HEAD. Use --sha to specify.", "red"))
    76→            return
    77→
    78→    # Determine which issues to record
    79→    uncommitted = get_uncommitted_issues(plan)
    80→    if not uncommitted:
    81→        print(colorize("  No uncommitted issues to record.", "yellow"))
    82→        return
    83→
    84→    if only_patterns:
    85→        issue_ids = filter_issue_ids_by_pattern(uncommitted, only_patterns)
    86→        if not issue_ids:
    87→            print(colorize("  No uncommitted issues match --only patterns.", "yellow"))
    88→            return
    89→    else:
    90→        issue_ids = None  # record all
    91→
    92→    record = record_commit(
    93→        plan,
    94→        sha=sha,
    95→        branch=branch,
    96→        issue_ids=issue_ids,
    97→        note=note,
    98→    )
    99→    append_log_entry(
   100→        plan,
   101→        "commit_record",
   102→        issue_ids=record["issue_ids"],
   103→        actor="user",
   104→        note=note,
   105→        detail={"sha": sha, "branch": branch},
   106→    )
   107→    save_plan(plan)
   108→
   109→    recorded = len(record["issue_ids"])
   110→    print(colorize(f"  Recorded commit {sha} with {recorded} issue(s).", "green"))
   111→    if note:
   112→        print(colorize(f"  Note: {note}", "dim"))
   113→
   114→    # Update PR if configured
   115→    config = load_config()
   116→    pr_number = config.get("commit_pr", 0)
   117→    if pr_number:
   118→        try:
   119→            state = state_mod.load_state()
   120→            body = generate_pr_body(plan, state)
   121→            ok = update_pr_body(pr_number, body)
   122→            if ok:
   123→                print(colorize(f"  PR #{pr_number} description updated.", "green"))
   124→            else:
   125→                print(colorize(f"  Could not update PR #{pr_number} (gh may not be available).", "yellow"))
   126→        except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as exc:
   127→            print(
   128→                colorize(f"  Warning: PR update skipped ({exc}).", "yellow"),
   129→                file=sys.stderr,
   130→            )
   131→
   132→
   133→def _cmd_commit_log_history(args: argparse.Namespace, plan: dict) -> None:
   134→    """Show commit records."""
   135→    top = getattr(args, "top", 10)
   136→    commit_log = plan.get("commit_log", [])
   137→
   138→    if not commit_log:
   139→        print(colorize("  No commits recorded yet.", "dim"))
   140→        return
   141→
   142→    print(colorize("  Commit History", "bold"))
   143→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 40, "dim"))
   144→
   145→    shown = commit_log[-top:] if top else commit_log
   146→    for record in reversed(shown):
   147→        sha = record.get("sha", "?")[:7]
   148→        branch = record.get("branch", "")
   149→        issue_ids = record.get("issue_ids", [])
   150→        note = record.get("note", "")
   151→        recorded_at = record.get("recorded_at", "")
   152→
   153→        header = f"  {sha}"
   154→        if branch:
   155→            header += f" ({branch})"
   156→        header += f" — {len(issue_ids)} issue(s)"
   157→        if recorded_at:
   158→            header += f"  [{recorded_at[:16]}]"
   159→        print(header)
   160→        if note:
   161→            print(colorize(f"    Note: {note}", "dim"))
   162→        for fid in issue_ids[:5]:
   163→            print(f"    - {fid}")
   164→        if len(issue_ids) > 5:
   165→            print(f"    ... and {len(issue_ids) - 5} more")
   166→
   167→
   168→def _cmd_commit_log_pr(plan: dict) -> None:
   169→    """Print PR body markdown to stdout (dry run)."""
   170→    try:
   171→        state = state_mod.load_state()
   172→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   173→        state = {"issues": {}}
   174→
   175→    body = generate_pr_body(plan, state)
   176→    print(body)
   177→
   178→
   179→_COMMIT_LOG_HANDLERS = {
   180→    "record": _cmd_commit_log_record,
   181→    "history": _cmd_commit_log_history,
   182→    "pr": lambda _args, plan: _cmd_commit_log_pr(plan),
   183→}
   184→
   185→
   186→def cmd_commit_log_dispatch(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   187→    """Route commit-log subcommands."""
   188→    config = load_config()
   189→    if not config.get("commit_tracking_enabled", True):
   190→        print(colorize("  Commit tracking is disabled. Enable with: desloppify config set commit_tracking_enabled true", "yellow"))
   191→        return
   192→
   193→    plan = load_plan()
   194→    action = getattr(args, "commit_log_action", None)
   195→    handler = _COMMIT_LOG_HANDLERS.get(action)
   196→    if handler is None:
   197→        _cmd_commit_log_status(plan)
   198→        return
   199→    handler(args, plan)
   200→
   201→
   202→__all__ = ["cmd_commit_log_dispatch"]
   203→
```

```
   104→def _seed_plan_start_scores(plan: dict[str, object], state: state_mod.StateModel) -> bool:
   105→    """Set plan_start_scores when beginning a new queue cycle."""
   106→    existing = plan.get("plan_start_scores")
```

Perfect! I've found all three files and identified the swallowed errors. Let me analyze each one:

## Summary of Swallowed Error Issues

### 1. **desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_parallel.py** (line 131)

**Location:** Lines 129-132
```python
try:
    safe_write_text(raw_path, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
except OSError as exc:
    logger.debug("Failed writing normalized batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
```

**What's being swallowed:**
- OSError exceptions during file write operations are caught and only logged at DEBUG level
- The execution continues silently without marking the payload write as failed or re-raising

**Appropriate fix:**
- This is actually **acceptable** as-is. The code logs the error at debug level, which is appropriate for a non-critical file normalization operation. The payload is already validated, and failure to write the normalized version doesn't prevent further processing. If stricter handling is needed, you could:
  - Mark the batch as requiring regeneration on next run
  - Log at WARNING instead of DEBUG if this is important for auditability

**Complexity:** Very Low — if a change is needed, just change `logger.debug` to `logger.warning`

---

### 2. **desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py** (line 334)

**Location:** Lines 291-335
```python
def reconcile_plan_post_scan(runtime: Any) -> None:
    """Reconcile plan queue metadata and stale subjective review dimensions."""
    try:
        # ... all the reconciliation logic (lines 292-333)
        if dirty:
            save_plan(plan, plan_path)
    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
        logger.warning("Plan reconciliation skipped: %s", exc)
```

**What's being swallowed:**
- The entire `reconcile_plan_post_scan()` function wraps all post-scan plan reconciliation in a broad try-except that catches `PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS`
- If ANY error in the reconciliation pipeline occurs (whether loading, processing, or saving), it logs a warning and silently continues
- The caller has no indication that reconciliation failed or was partial

**Appropriate fix:**
- The broad exception handling **loses context** about which reconciliation step failed (apply, sync_unscored, sync_stale, auto_clusters, triage, communicate_score, create_plan, start_scores, or save)
- Better approach: wrap individual high-risk operations (plan load/save) and let internal operations propagate errors, or use granular error context like:
  ```python
  try:
      plan = load_plan(plan_path)
  except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
      logger.warning("Cannot load plan for reconciliation: %s", exc)
      return
  # ... all the sync operations without top-level catch
  try:
      save_plan(plan, plan_path)
  except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
      logger.warning("Failed to save reconciled plan: %s", exc)
  ```
- Or add detailed logging before each major step so if it fails, the warning includes context

**Complexity:** Medium — requires refactoring the exception handling to provide better error attribution and granularity

---

### 3. **desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py** (line 62)

**Location:** Lines 57-64
```python
def _workflow_stage_index(item: WorkQueueItem) -> int:
    raw_index = item.get("stage_index")
    if raw_index is not None:
        try:
            return int(raw_index)
        except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
            logger.debug("Invalid workflow stage index %r: %s", raw_index, exc)
    return _TRIAGE_STAGE_ORDER.get(workflow_stage_name(item).lower(), 0)
```

**What's being swallowed:**
- TypeError or ValueError from `int(raw_index)` conversion are caught and logged at DEBUG level
- The function then falls back to deriving the index from the workflow stage name

**Appropriate fix:**
- This is **acceptable as-is**. It's a defensive fallback pattern:
  - Tries the fast path (direct integer conversion)
  - Falls back to slower path (derive from stage name) if data is malformed
  - Logs at appropriate DEBUG level for troubleshooting
- If you want to be stricter, you could:
  - Log at WARNING level instead of DEBUG
  - Validate `stage_index` is numeric when writing it to the queue
  - But this would be premature — the current pattern gracefully handles data inconsistencies

**Complexity:** Very Low — acceptable as-is, or minimal change if you want DEBUG→WARNING

---

## Summary Table

| File | Line | Issue | Severity | Fix Complexity |
|------|------|-------|----------|-----------------|
| runner_parallel.py | 131 | DEBUG-only logging on OSError during payload write | **Low** | Very Low (if needed) |
| plan_reconcile.py | 334 | Broad try-except swallows all reconciliation errors without context | **Medium** | Medium (requires refactoring) |
| ranking.py | 62 | DEBUG-only logging on type/value errors with graceful fallback | **Low** | Very Low (if needed) |

The **primary concern** is **plan_reconcile.py**, where the broad exception handler at the function level loses diagnostic information about which reconciliation step failed and why.

```
1→"""exclude command: add path patterns and clean stale queue/state entries."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    11→from desloppify.base import config as config_mod
    12→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS, CommandError
    13→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion
    14→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    15→from desloppify.base.tooling import check_config_staleness
    16→from desloppify.engine.plan import (
    17→    load_plan,
    18→    plan_path_for_state,
    19→    purge_ids,
    20→    save_plan,
    21→)
    22→
    23→
    24→def _state_file_for_runtime(runtime) -> Path:
    25→    """Resolve the effective state file path for a command runtime."""
    26→    state_file = runtime.state_path
    27→    if isinstance(state_file, Path):
    28→        return state_file
    29→    return state_mod.STATE_FILE
    30→
    31→
    32→def _prune_excluded_issues(state: dict, pattern: str) -> list[str]:
    33→    """Drop issues whose file path matches a new exclusion pattern."""
    34→    issues = state.get("issues")
    35→    if not isinstance(issues, dict):
    36→        return []
    37→
    38→    removed_ids = [
    39→        issue_id
    40→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items()
    41→        if isinstance(issue, dict)
    42→        and matches_exclusion(str(issue.get("file", "")), pattern)
    43→    ]
    44→    for issue_id in removed_ids:
    45→        issues.pop(issue_id, None)
    46→    return removed_ids
    47→
    48→
    49→def _purge_removed_ids_from_plan(state_file: Path, removed_ids: list[str]) -> int:
    50→    """Remove pruned issue IDs from queue/skips/clusters in plan.json."""
    51→    if not removed_ids:
    52→        return 0
    53→    plan_file = plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    54→    plan = load_plan(plan_file)
    55→    purged = purge_ids(plan, removed_ids)
    56→    if purged:
    57→        save_plan(plan, plan_file)
    58→    return purged
    59→
    60→
    61→def cmd_exclude(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    62→    """Add a path pattern to the exclude list and clean cached queue/state."""
    63→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    64→    config = runtime.config
    65→    state = runtime.state
    66→    state_file = _state_file_for_runtime(runtime)
    67→
    68→    config_mod.add_exclude_pattern(config, args.pattern)
    69→    config["needs_rescan"] = True
    70→    try:
    71→        config_mod.save_config(config)
    72→    except OSError as exc:
    73→        raise CommandError(f"could not save config: {exc}") from exc
    74→
    75→    removed_ids: list[str] = []
    76→    plan_purged = 0
    77→    if state_file.exists():
    78→        removed_ids = _prune_excluded_issues(state, args.pattern)
    79→        if removed_ids:
    80→            try:
    81→                state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)
    82→            except OSError as exc:
    83→                raise CommandError(f"could not save state: {exc}") from exc
    84→
    85→            try:
    86→                plan_purged = _purge_removed_ids_from_plan(state_file, removed_ids)
    87→            except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
    88→                print(
    89→                    colorize("  Warning: could not update living plan.", "yellow"),
    90→                    file=sys.stderr,
    91→                )
    92→
    93→    print(colorize(f"Added exclude pattern: {args.pattern}", "green"))
    94→    if removed_ids:
    95→        print(f"  Removed {len(removed_ids)} matching issues from state.")
    96→        if plan_purged:
    97→            print(
    98→                colorize(
    99→                    f"  Plan updated: {plan_purged} item(s) removed from queue.",
   100→                    "dim",
   101→                )
   102→            )
   103→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
   104→    if config_warning:
   105→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
   106→
   107→
   108→__all__ = ["cmd_exclude"]
   109→
```

```
1→"""Path resolution and exclusion matching helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import os
     7→import tempfile
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    11→
    12→
    13→def matches_exclusion(rel_path: str, exclusion: str) -> bool:
    14→    """Check if a relative path matches an exclusion pattern."""
    15→    parts = Path(rel_path).parts
    16→    if exclusion in parts:
    17→        return True
    18→    if "*" in exclusion:
    19→        if any(fnmatch.fnmatch(part, exclusion) for part in parts):
    20→            return True
    21→        # Full-path glob match for patterns with directory separators
    22→        # (e.g. "Wan2GP/**" should match "Wan2GP/models/rf.py").
    23→        if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
    24→            normalized_path = rel_path.lstrip("./")
    25→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(normalized_path, exclusion):
    26→                return True
    27→    if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
    28→        normalized = exclusion.rstrip("/").rstrip(os.sep)
    29→        return rel_path == normalized or rel_path.startswith(normalized + "/") or rel_path.startswith(
    30→            normalized + os.sep
    31→        )
    32→    return False
    33→
    34→
    35→def normalize_path_separators(path: str) -> str:
    36→    return path.replace("\\", "/")
    37→
    38→
    39→def safe_relpath(path: str | Path, start: str | Path) -> str:
    40→    try:
    41→        return os.path.relpath(str(path), str(start))
    42→    except ValueError:
    43→        return str(Path(path).resolve())
    44→
    45→
    46→def rel(path: str) -> str:
    47→    root = get_project_root()
    48→    resolved = Path(path).resolve()
    49→    try:
    50→        return normalize_path_separators(str(resolved.relative_to(root)))
    51→    except ValueError:
    52→        return normalize_path_separators(safe_relpath(resolved, root))
    53→
    54→
    55→def resolve_path(filepath: str) -> str:
    56→    """Resolve a filepath to absolute, handling both relative and absolute."""
    57→    p = Path(filepath)
    58→    if p.is_absolute():
    59→        return str(p.resolve())
    60→    return str((get_project_root() / filepath).resolve())
    61→
    62→
    63→def resolve_scan_file(
    64→    filepath: str | Path,
    65→    *,
    66→    scan_root: str | Path | None = None,
    67→) -> Path:
    68→    """Resolve a scan file path with explicit scan-root-first semantics.
    69→
    70→    Relative file paths are resolved against ``scan_root`` first (when provided)
    71→    and then against the process project root as a fallback.
    72→    """
    73→    p = Path(filepath)
    74→    if p.is_absolute():
    75→        return p.resolve()
    76→
    77→    root = get_project_root()
    78→    if scan_root is not None:
    79→        scan_root_path = Path(scan_root)
    80→        scan_root_abs = (
    81→            scan_root_path.resolve()
    82→            if scan_root_path.is_absolute()
    83→            else (root / scan_root_path).resolve()
    84→        )
    85→        scan_candidate = (scan_root_abs / p).resolve()
    86→        if scan_candidate.exists():
    87→            return scan_candidate
    88→
    89→    return (root / p).resolve()
    90→
    91→
    92→def safe_write_text(filepath: str | Path, content: str) -> None:
    93→    """Atomically write text to a file using temp+rename."""
    94→    p = Path(filepath)
    95→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    96→    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=p.parent, suffix=".tmp")
    97→    try:
    98→        with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as f:
    99→            f.write(content)
   100→        os.replace(tmp, str(p))
   101→    except OSError:
   102→        if os.path.exists(tmp):
   103→            os.unlink(tmp)
   104→        raise
   105→
   106→
   107→def count_lines(path: Path) -> int:
   108→    """Count lines in a file without loading full contents into memory."""
   109→    count = 0
   110→    try:
   111→        with path.open("rb") as handle:
   112→            for _ in handle:
   113→                count += 1
   114→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
   115→        return 0
   116→    return count
   117→
   118→
   119→__all__ = [
   120→    "count_lines",
   121→    "matches_exclusion",
   122→    "normalize_path_separators",
   123→    "rel",
   124→    "resolve_path",
   125→    "resolve_scan_file",
   126→    "safe_relpath",
   127→    "safe_write_text",
   128→]
   129→
```

```
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→

     6→import sys

     9→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser import create_parser as _create_parser
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import LangResolutionError, resolve_lang
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import CommandRuntime
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path

    18→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize

    20→from desloppify.base.registry import detector_names, on_detector_registered
    21→from desloppify.base.runtime_state import runtime_scope
    22→from desloppify.languages import available_langs
    23→from desloppify.state import load_state
    24→
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→
    27→
    28→class _DetectorNamesCacheCompat:
    29→    """Compat shim for tests that poke the legacy detector-name cache."""
    30→
    31→    def __init__(self) -> None:
    32→        self._store: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    33→
    34→    def __contains__(self, key: object) -> bool:
    35→        return key in self._store
    36→
    37→    def __getitem__(self, key: str) -> list[str]:
    38→        return self._store[key]
    39→
    40→    def __setitem__(self, key: str, value: list[str]) -> None:
    41→        self._store[key] = value
    42→
    43→    def pop(self, key: str, default=None):
    44→        return self._store.pop(key, default)
    45→
    46→
    47→_DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE = _DetectorNamesCacheCompat()
    48→
    49→
    50→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
    51→def _get_detector_names_cached() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    52→    """Compute detector names once until cache invalidation."""
    53→    return tuple(detector_names())
    54→
    55→
    56→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    57→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    58→    return list(_get_detector_names_cached())
    59→
    60→
    61→def _invalidate_detector_names_cache() -> None:
    62→    """Invalidate detector-name cache when runtime registrations change."""
    63→    _get_detector_names_cached.cache_clear()
    64→    _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE.pop("names", None)
    65→
    66→
    67→on_detector_registered(_invalidate_detector_names_cache)
    68→
    69→
    70→def create_parser():
    71→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    72→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
    73→
    74→
    75→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config: dict):
    76→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    77→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    78→    persisted = config.get("exclude", [])
    79→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    80→    if not combined:
    81→        return
    82→    set_exclusions(combined)
    83→    if cli_exclusions:
    84→        print(
    85→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    86→            file=sys.stderr,
    87→        )
    88→        return
    89→    print(
    90→        colorize(
    91→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
    92→        ),
    93→        file=sys.stderr,
    94→    )
    95→
    96→
    97→def _resolve_default_path(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    98→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
    99→
   100→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
   101→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
   102→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
   103→    """
   104→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
   105→        return
   106→    runtime_root = get_project_root()
   107→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
   108→        try:
   109→            state_file = state_path(args)
   110→            if state_file:
   111→                saved = load_state(state_file)
   112→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
   113→                if saved_path:
   114→                    args.path = str((runtime_root / saved_path).resolve())
   115→                    return
   116→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
   117→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, "resolve default review path from saved state", exc)
   118→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   119→    if lang:
   120→        args.path = str(runtime_root / lang.default_src)
   121→    else:
   122→        args.path = str(get_default_path())
   123→
   124→
   125→def _load_shared_runtime(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   126→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
   127→    config = load_config()
   128→
   129→    state_file = state_path(args)
   130→    state = load_state(state_file)
   131→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   132→
   133→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
   134→
   135→
   136→def _resolve_handler(command: str):
   137→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   138→
   139→
   140→def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
   141→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   142→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   143→    try:
   144→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   145→    except SystemExit:
   146→        return
   147→
   148→
   149→def main() -> None:
   150→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   151→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   152→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   153→            try:
   154→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   155→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   156→                logger.debug(
   157→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   158→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   159→                )
   160→
   161→    parser = create_parser()
   162→    args = parser.parse_args()
   163→    if args.command == "help":
   164→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   165→        return
   166→
   167→    try:
   168→        with runtime_scope():
   169→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   170→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   171→
   172→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   173→            handler(args)
   174→    except CommandError as exc:
   175→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   176→        sys.exit(exc.exit_code)
   177→    except LangResolutionError as exc:
   178→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   179→        sys.exit(1)
   180→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   181→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   182→        sys.exit(1)
   183→
   184→
   185→if __name__ == "__main__":
   186→    main()
   187→
```

```
   119→
   120→
   121→def default_config() -> dict[str, Any]:
   122→    """Return a config dict with all keys set to their defaults."""
   123→    return {k: copy.deepcopy(v.default) for k, v in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items()}
   124→
   125→
   126→def _coerce_target_strict_score(value: object) -> tuple[int, bool]:
   127→    """Coerce target strict score and report whether it is in range."""
   128→    try:
   129→        parsed = int(value)
   130→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   131→        return MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE, False
   132→    valid = MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE <= parsed <= MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   133→    return parsed, valid
   134→
   135→
   136→def _load_config_payload(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
   137→    if path.exists():
   138→        try:
   139→            payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
   140→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
   141→            return {}
   142→        return payload if isinstance(payload, dict) else {}
   143→    # First run — try migrating from state files
   144→    return _migrate_from_state_files(path)
   145→
   146→
   147→def _migrate_legacy_noise_keys(config: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   148→    changed = False
   149→    for old, new in (
   150→        ("finding_noise_budget", "issue_noise_budget"),
   151→        ("finding_noise_global_budget", "issue_noise_global_budget"),
   152→    ):
   153→        changed |= _rename_key(config, old, new)
   154→    return changed
   155→
   156→
   157→def _apply_schema_defaults_and_normalization(config: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   158→    changed = False
   159→    for key, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items():
   160→        if key not in config:
   161→            config[key] = copy.deepcopy(schema.default)
   162→            changed = True
   163→            continue
   164→        if key != "badge_path":
   165→            continue
   166→        try:
   167→            normalized = _validate_badge_path(str(config[key]))
   168→            if normalized != config[key]:
   169→                config[key] = normalized
   170→                changed = True
   171→        except ValueError:
   172→            config[key] = copy.deepcopy(schema.default)
   173→            changed = True
   174→    return changed
   175→
   176→
   177→def load_config(path: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
   178→    """Load config from disk, auto-migrating from state files if needed.
   179→
   180→    Fills missing keys with defaults. If no config.json exists, attempts
   181→    migration from state-*.json files.
   182→    """
   183→    p = path or _default_config_file()
   184→    config = _load_config_payload(p)
   185→    changed = _migrate_legacy_noise_keys(config)
   186→    changed |= _apply_schema_defaults_and_normalization(config)
   187→
   188→    if changed and p.exists():
   189→        try:
   190→            save_config(config, p)
   191→        except OSError as exc:
   192→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"persist migrated config to {p}", exc)
   193→
   194→    return config
   195→
   196→
   197→def save_config(config: dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
   198→    """Save config to disk atomically."""
   199→    p = path or _default_config_file()
   200→    safe_write_text(p, json.dumps(config, indent=2) + "\n")
   201→
   202→
   203→def add_ignore_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
   204→    """Append a pattern to the ignore list (deduplicates)."""
   205→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   206→    if pattern not in ignores:
   207→        ignores.append(pattern)
   208→
   209→
   210→def add_exclude_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
   211→    """Append a pattern to the exclude list (deduplicates)."""
   212→    excludes = config.setdefault("exclude", [])
   213→    if pattern not in excludes:
   214→        excludes.append(pattern)
   215→
   216→
   217→def set_ignore_metadata(config: dict, pattern: str, *, note: str, added_at: str) -> None:
   218→    """Record note + timestamp for an ignore pattern."""
   219→    meta = config.setdefault("ignore_metadata", {})
   220→    if not isinstance(meta, dict):
   221→        meta = {}
   222→        config["ignore_metadata"] = meta
   223→    meta[pattern] = {"note": note, "added_at": added_at}
   224→
   225→
   226→def _validate_badge_path(raw: str) -> str:
   227→    """Require badge_path to point to a filename (root or nested path)."""
   228→    value = raw.strip()
   229→    path = Path(value)
   230→    if (
   231→        not value
   232→        or value.endswith(("/", "\\"))
   233→        or path.name in {"", ".", ".."}
   234→    ):
   235→        raise ValueError(
   236→            "Expected file path for badge_path "
   237→            f"(example: scorecard.png or assets/scorecard.png), got: {raw}"
   238→        )
   239→    return value
   240→
   241→
   242→def _set_int_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   243→    if raw.lower() == "never":
   244→        config[key] = 0
   245→    else:
   246→        config[key] = int(raw)
   247→    if key != "target_strict_score":
   248→        return
   249→    target_strict_score, target_valid = _coerce_target_strict_score(config[key])
   250→    if not target_valid:
   251→        raise ValueError(
   252→            f"Expected integer {MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE}-{MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE} "
   253→            f"for {key}, got: {raw}"
   254→        )
   255→    config[key] = target_strict_score
   256→
   257→
   258→def _set_bool_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   259→    normalized = raw.lower()
   260→    if normalized in ("true", "1", "yes"):
   261→        config[key] = True
   262→        return
   263→    if normalized in ("false", "0", "no"):
   264→        config[key] = False
   265→        return
   266→    raise ValueError(f"Expected true/false for {key}, got: {raw}")
   267→
   268→
   269→def _set_str_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   270→    if key == "badge_path":
   271→        config[key] = _validate_badge_path(raw)
   272→        return
   273→    config[key] = raw
   274→
   275→
   276→def _set_list_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   277→    config.setdefault(key, [])
   278→    if raw not in config[key]:
   279→        config[key].append(raw)
   280→
   281→
   282→_SCHEMA_SETTERS = {
   283→    int: _set_int_config_value,
   284→    bool: _set_bool_config_value,
   285→    str: _set_str_config_value,
   286→    list: _set_list_config_value,
   287→}
   288→
   289→
   290→def set_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   291→    """Parse and set a config value from a raw string.
   292→
   293→    Handles special cases:
   294→    - "never" → 0 for age keys
   295→    - "true"/"false" for bools
   296→    """
   297→    if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   298→        raise KeyError(f"Unknown config key: {key}")
   299→
   300→    schema = CONFIG_SCHEMA[key]
   301→    setter = _SCHEMA_SETTERS.get(schema.type)
   302→    if setter is not None:
   303→        setter(config, key, raw)
   304→        return
   305→    if schema.type is dict:
   306→        raise ValueError(f"Cannot set dict key '{key}' via CLI — use subcommands")
   307→    config[key] = raw
   308→
   309→
   310→def unset_config_value(config: dict, key: str) -> None:
   311→    """Reset a config key to its default value."""
   312→    if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   313→        raise KeyError(f"Unknown config key: {key}")
   314→    config[key] = copy.deepcopy(CONFIG_SCHEMA[key].default)
   315→
   316→
   317→def config_for_query(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   318→    """Return a sanitized config dict suitable for query.json."""
   319→    return {k: config.get(k, schema.default) for k, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items()}
   320→
   321→
   322→def _merge_config_value(config: dict, key: str, value: object) -> None:
   323→    """Merge a config value into the target dict."""
   324→    if key not in config:
   325→        config[key] = copy.deepcopy(value)
   326→        return
   327→    if isinstance(value, list) and isinstance(config[key], list):
   328→        for item in value:
   329→            if item not in config[key]:
   330→                config[key].append(item)
   331→        return
   332→    if isinstance(value, dict) and isinstance(config[key], dict):
   333→        for dk, dv in value.items():
   334→            if dk not in config[key]:
   335→                config[key][dk] = copy.deepcopy(dv)
   336→        return
   337→
   338→
   339→def _load_state_file_payload(path: Path) -> dict | None:
   340→    try:
   341→        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
   342→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   343→        logger.debug("Skipping unreadable state file %s: %s", path, exc)
   344→        return None
   345→    if isinstance(payload, dict):
   346→        return payload
   347→    return None
   348→
   349→
   350→def _merge_legacy_state_config(config: dict, old_config: dict) -> None:
   351→    for key, value in old_config.items():
   352→        if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   353→            continue
   354→        _merge_config_value(config, key, value)
   355→
   356→
   357→def _strip_config_from_state_file(path: Path, state_data: dict) -> None:
   358→    if "config" not in state_data:
   359→        return
   360→    del state_data["config"]
   361→    try:
   362→        safe_write_text(path, json.dumps(state_data, indent=2) + "\n")
   363→    except OSError as exc:
   364→        log_best_effort_failure(
   365→            logger,
   366→            f"rewrite state file {path} after config migration",
   367→            exc,
   368→        )
   369→
   370→
   371→def _migrate_single_state_file(config: dict, path: Path) -> bool:
   372→    state_data = _load_state_file_payload(path)
   373→    if state_data is None:
   374→        return False
   375→    old_config = state_data.get("config")
   376→    if not isinstance(old_config, dict) or not old_config:
   377→        return False
   378→
   379→    _merge_legacy_state_config(config, old_config)
   380→    _strip_config_from_state_file(path, state_data)
   381→    return True
   382→
   383→
   384→def _migrate_from_state_files(config_path: Path) -> dict:
   385→    """Migrate config keys from state-*.json files into config.json.
   386→
   387→    Reads state["config"] from all state files, merges them (union for
   388→    lists, merge for dicts), writes config.json, and strips "config" from
   389→    the state files.
   390→    """
   391→    config: dict = {}
   392→    state_dir = config_path.parent
   393→    if not state_dir.exists():
   394→        return config
   395→
   396→    state_files = list(state_dir.glob("state-*.json")) + list(
   397→        state_dir.glob("state.json")
   398→    )
   399→    migrated_any = False
   400→    for sf in state_files:
   401→        migrated_any = _migrate_single_state_file(config, sf) or migrated_any
   402→
   403→    if migrated_any and config:
   404→        try:
   405→            save_config(config, config_path)
   406→        except OSError as exc:
   407→            log_best_effort_failure(
   408→                logger, f"save migrated config to {config_path}", exc
   409→            )
   410→
   411→    return config
   412→
   413→
   414→# ── Target score helpers ─────────────────────────────────────
   415→
   416→
   417→def coerce_target_score(
   418→    value: object, *, fallback: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   419→) -> float:
   420→    """Normalize target score-like values to a safe [0, 100] float."""
   421→    if is_numeric(fallback):
   422→        fallback_value = float(fallback)
   423→    else:
   424→        fallback_value = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   425→
   426→    if is_numeric(value):
   427→        parsed = float(value)
   428→    elif isinstance(value, str):
   429→        text = value.strip()
   430→        if not text:
   431→            parsed = fallback_value
   432→        else:
   433→            try:
   434→                parsed = float(text)
   435→            except ValueError:
   436→                parsed = fallback_value
   437→    else:
   438→        parsed = fallback_value
   439→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, parsed))
   440→
   441→
   442→def target_strict_score_from_config(
   443→    config: dict | None, *, fallback: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   444→) -> float:
   445→    """Read and normalize target strict score from config."""
   446→    if isinstance(config, dict):
   447→        raw = config.get("target_strict_score", fallback)
   448→    else:
   449→        raw = fallback
   450→    return coerce_target_score(raw, fallback=fallback)
   451→
```

```
    36→def print_stage_progress(stages: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> None:
    37→    """Print the 4-stage progress indicator."""
    38→    print(colorize("  Stages:", "dim"))
    39→    for stage_name, label in TRIAGE_STAGE_LABELS:
    40→        if stage_name in stages:
    41→            if stages[stage_name].get("confirmed_at"):
    42→                print(colorize(f"    \u2713 {label} (confirmed)", "green"))
    43→            else:
    44→                print(colorize(f"    \u2713 {label} (needs confirm)", "yellow"))
    45→        elif TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES[stage_name].issubset(stages):
    46→            print(colorize(f"    \u2192 {label} (current)", "yellow"))
    47→        else:
    48→            print(colorize(f"    \u25cb {label}", "dim"))
    49→
    50→    # Show enrichment gaps when in the organize stage
    51→    if plan and "reflect" in stages and "organize" not in stages:
    52→        gaps = _unenriched_clusters(plan)
    53→        manual = manual_clusters_with_issues(plan)
    54→        if not manual:
    55→            print(colorize("\n    No manual clusters yet. Create clusters and enrich them.", "yellow"))
    56→        elif gaps:
    57→            print(colorize(f"\n    {len(gaps)} cluster(s) need enrichment:", "yellow"))
    58→            for name, missing in gaps:
    59→                print(colorize(f"      {name}: missing {', '.join(missing)}", "yellow"))
    60→            print(colorize(
    61→                f"      Fix: {TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_ENRICH_COMPACT}",
    62→                "dim",
    63→            ))
    64→        else:
    65→            print(colorize(f"\n    All {len(manual)} manual cluster(s) enriched.", "green"))
    66→
    67→def print_progress(plan: dict, open_issues: dict) -> None:
    68→    """Show cluster state and unclustered issues."""
    69→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    70→    # Only show clusters that actually have issues (hide empty/stale ones)
    71→    active_clusters = {
    72→        name: c for name, c in clusters.items()
    73→        if c.get("issue_ids")
    74→    }
    75→    if active_clusters:
    76→        print(colorize("\n  Current clusters:", "cyan"))
    77→        for name, cluster in active_clusters.items():
    78→            count = len(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    79→            desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
    80→            steps = cluster.get("action_steps", [])
    81→            auto = cluster.get("auto", False)
    82→            tags: list[str] = []
    83→            if auto:
    84→                tags.append("auto")
    85→            if desc:
    86→                tags.append("desc")
    87→            else:
    88→                tags.append("no desc")
    89→            if steps:
    90→                tags.append(f"{len(steps)} steps")
    91→            else:
    92→                if not auto:
    93→                    tags.append("no steps")
    94→            tag_str = f" [{', '.join(tags)}]"
    95→            desc_str = f" \u2014 {desc}" if desc else ""
    96→            print(f"    {name}: {count} items{tag_str}{desc_str}")
    97→
    98→    all_clustered: set[str] = set()
    99→    for c in clusters.values():
   100→        all_clustered.update(c.get("issue_ids", []))
   101→    unclustered = [fid for fid in open_issues if fid not in all_clustered]
   102→    if unclustered:
   103→        print(colorize(f"\n  {len(unclustered)} issues not yet in a cluster:", "yellow"))
   104→        for fid in unclustered[:10]:
   105→            f = open_issues[fid]
   106→            dim = (f.get("detail", {}) or {}).get("dimension", "") if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else ""
   107→            short = short_issue_id(fid)
   108→            print(f"    [{short}] [{dim}] {f.get('summary', '')}")
   109→        if len(unclustered) > 10:
   110→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(unclustered) - 10} more", "dim"))
   111→    elif open_issues:
   112→        organized, total, _ = triage_coverage(plan, open_review_ids=set(open_issues.keys()))
   113→        print(colorize(f"\n  All {organized}/{total} issues are in clusters.", "green"))
   114→
   115→def print_reflect_result(
   116→    *,
   117→    issue_count: int,
   118→    recurring_dims: list[str],
   119→    recurring: dict,
   120→    report: str,
   121→    is_reuse: bool,
   122→    cleared: list,
   123→    stages: dict,
   124→) -> None:
   125→    """Print the reflect stage output including briefing box and next steps."""
   126→    print(colorize(
   127→        f"  Reflect stage recorded: {issue_count} issues, "
   128→        f"{len(recurring_dims)} recurring dimension(s).",
   129→        "green",
   130→    ))
   131→    if is_reuse:
   132→        print(colorize("  Reflect data preserved (no changes).", "dim"))
   133→        if cleared:
   134→            print_cascade_clear_feedback(cleared, stages)
   135→    else:
   136→        print(colorize("  Now confirm your strategy.", "yellow"))
   137→        print(colorize("    desloppify plan triage --confirm reflect", "dim"))
   138→    if recurring_dims:
   139→        for dim in recurring_dims:
   140→            info = recurring[dim]
   141→            print(colorize(
   142→                f"    {dim}: {len(info['resolved'])} resolved, {len(info['open'])} still open",
   143→                "dim",
   144→            ))
   145→
   146→    print()
   147→    print(colorize("  \u250c\u2500 Strategic briefing (share with user before organizing) \u2500\u2510", "cyan"))
   148→    for line in report.strip().splitlines():
   149→        print(colorize(f"  \u2502 {line}", "cyan"))
   150→    print(colorize("  \u2514" + "\u2500" * 57 + "\u2518", "cyan"))
   151→    print_user_message(
   152→        "Hey — reflect is recorded, your strategy is printed"
   153→        " above. Before you confirm, make sure it's thorough —"
   154→        " think through contradictions, decide what's worth doing"
   155→        " vs busywork, lay out the real shape of the work. Write"
   156→        " it up properly if it helps. Once you're confident,"
   157→        " confirm and start organizing: create clusters, enrich"
   158→        " them, then record organize. No need to wait for my"
   159→        " input unless I've asked you to."
   160→    )
   161→
   162→def print_organize_result(
   163→    *,
   164→    manual_clusters: list[str],
   165→    plan: dict,
   166→    report: str,
   167→    is_reuse: bool,
   168→    cleared: list,
   169→    stages: dict,
   170→) -> None:
   171→    """Print the organize stage output including cluster summary and next steps."""
   172→    print(colorize(
   173→        f"  Organize stage recorded: {len(manual_clusters)} enriched cluster(s).",
   174→        "green",
   175→    ))
   176→    if is_reuse:
   177→        print(colorize("  Organize data preserved (no changes).", "dim"))
   178→        if cleared:
   179→            print_cascade_clear_feedback(cleared, stages)
   180→    else:
   181→        print(colorize("  Now confirm the plan.", "yellow"))
   182→        print(colorize("    desloppify plan triage --confirm organize", "dim"))
   183→    for name in manual_clusters:
   184→        cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(name, {})
   185→        steps = cluster.get("action_steps", [])
   186→        desc = cluster.get("description", "")
   187→        desc_str = f" \u2014 {desc}" if desc else ""
   188→        print(colorize(f"    {name}: {len(cluster.get('issue_ids', []))} issues, {len(steps)} steps{desc_str}", "dim"))
   189→
   190→    print()
   191→    print(colorize("  \u250c\u2500 Prioritized organization (share with user) \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510", "cyan"))
   192→    for line in report.strip().splitlines():
   193→        print(colorize(f"  \u2502 {line}", "cyan"))
   194→    print(colorize("  \u2514" + "\u2500" * 57 + "\u2518", "cyan"))
   195→    print_user_message(
   196→        "Hey — organize is recorded, your clusters are printed"
   197→        " above. Before you confirm, think from the executor's"
   198→        " perspective: is every task code-monkey-proof? Are the"
   199→        " action steps detailed enough that someone with zero"
   200→        " context won't make mistakes? Is the sequencing right"
   201→        " — both across clusters and within each one? Research"
   202→        " anything you're unsure about. Once you're confident,"
   203→        " confirm and complete triage. No need to stop unless"
   204→        " I've asked you to."
   205→    )
   206→
   207→def print_reflect_dashboard(
   208→    si: object,
   209→    plan: dict,
   210→    *,
   211→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   212→) -> None:
   213→    """Show completed clusters, resolved issues, and recurring patterns."""
   214→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   215→    # si is a TriageInput
   216→    completed = getattr(si, "completed_clusters", [])
   217→    resolved = getattr(si, "resolved_issues", {})
   218→    open_issues = getattr(si, "open_issues", {})
   219→
   220→    if completed:
   221→        print(colorize("\n  Previously completed clusters:", "cyan"))
   222→        for c in completed[:10]:
   223→            name = c.get("name", "?")
   224→            count = len(c.get("issue_ids", []))
   225→            thesis = c.get("thesis", "")
   226→            print(f"    {name}: {count} issues")
   227→            if thesis:
   228→                print(colorize(f"      {thesis}", "dim"))
   229→            for step in c.get("action_steps", [])[:3]:
   230→                print(colorize(f"      - {step}", "dim"))
   231→        if len(completed) > 10:
   232→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(completed) - 10} more", "dim"))
   233→
   234→    if resolved:
   235→        print(colorize(f"\n  Resolved issues since last triage: {len(resolved)}", "cyan"))
   236→        for fid, f in sorted(resolved.items())[:10]:
   237→            status = f.get("status", "")
   238→            summary = f.get("summary", "")
   239→            detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   240→            dim = detail.get("dimension", "")
   241→            print(f"    [{status}] [{dim}] {summary}")
   242→            print(colorize(f"      {fid}", "dim"))
   243→        if len(resolved) > 10:
   244→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(resolved) - 10} more", "dim"))
   245→
   246→    recurring = resolved_services.detect_recurring_patterns(open_issues, resolved)
   247→    if recurring:
   248→        print(colorize("\n  Recurring patterns detected:", "yellow"))
   249→        for dim, info in sorted(recurring.items()):
   250→            resolved_count = len(info["resolved"])
   251→            open_count = len(info["open"])
   252→            label = "potential loop" if open_count >= resolved_count else "root cause unaddressed"
   253→            print(colorize(
   254→                f"    {dim}: {resolved_count} resolved, {open_count} still open — {label}",
   255→                "yellow",
   256→            ))
   257→    elif not completed and not resolved:
   258→        print(colorize("\n  First triage — no prior work to compare against.", "dim"))
   259→        print(colorize("  Focus your reflect report on your strategy:", "yellow"))
   260→        print(colorize("  - How will you resolve contradictions you identified in observe?", "dim"))
   261→        print(colorize("  - Which issues will you cluster together vs defer?", "dim"))
   262→        print(colorize("  - What's the overall arc of work and why?", "dim"))
   263→
   264→def _print_dashboard_header(si: object, stages: dict, meta: dict, plan: dict) -> None:
   265→    """Print the header section: title, open issues count, stage progress, overall status."""
   266→    print(colorize("  Epic triage \u2014 manual", "bold"))
   267→    print(colorize("  " + "\u2500" * 60, "dim"))
   268→    print(f"  Open review issues: {len(si.open_issues)}")
   269→    print(colorize("  Goal: identify contradictions, resolve them, then group the coherent", "cyan"))
   270→    print(colorize("  remainder into clusters by root cause with action steps and priorities.", "cyan"))
   271→    if si.existing_epics:
   272→        print(f"  Existing epics: {len(si.existing_epics)}")
   273→    if si.new_since_last:
   274→        print(colorize(f"  New since last triage: {len(si.new_since_last)}", "yellow"))
   275→        for fid in sorted(si.new_since_last):
   276→            f = si.open_issues.get(fid, {})
   277→            dim = ""
   278→            detail = f.get("detail")
   279→            if isinstance(detail, dict):
   280→                dim = detail.get("dimension", "")
   281→            dim_tag = f" ({dim})" if dim else ""
   282→            print(colorize(f"    * [{short_issue_id(fid)}] {f.get('summary', '')}{dim_tag}", "yellow"))
   283→    if si.resolved_since_last:
   284→        print(f"  Resolved since last triage: {len(si.resolved_since_last)}")
   285→
   286→    # Stage progress (with enrichment gaps)
   287→    print()
   288→    print_stage_progress(stages, plan)
   289→    if meta.get("stage_refresh_required"):
   290→        print(
   291→            colorize(
   292→                "  Note: review issues changed since stage progress started; "
   293→                "refresh stage reports before completion.",
   294→                "yellow",
   295→            )
   296→        )
   297→
   298→
   299→def _print_action_guidance(stages: dict, meta: dict, si: object, plan: dict) -> None:
   300→    """Print the 'What to do' action guidance section based on current stage."""
   301→    print()
   302→    has_only_additions = bool(si.new_since_last) and not si.resolved_since_last
   303→    if "observe" not in stages and has_only_additions and meta.get("strategy_summary"):
   304→        # Show both paths: accept or re-plan
   305→        print(colorize("  Two paths available:", "yellow"))
   306→        print()
   307→        print(colorize("  To accept current queue (new items at end):", "cyan"))
   308→        print(
   309→            '    desloppify plan triage --confirm-existing '
   310→            '--note "..." --strategy "same" --confirmed "I have reviewed..."'
   311→        )
   312→        print()
   313→        print(colorize("  To re-prioritize and restructure:", "cyan"))
   314→        print(f"    {TRIAGE_CMD_OBSERVE}")
   315→    elif "observe" not in stages:
   316→        print(colorize("  Next step:", "yellow"))
   317→        print(f"    {TRIAGE_CMD_OBSERVE}")
   318→        print(colorize("    (themes, root causes, contradictions between issues — NOT a list of IDs)", "dim"))
   319→    elif "reflect" not in stages:
   320→        print(colorize("  Next step: use the completed work and patterns below to write your reflect report.", "yellow"))
   321→        print(f"    {TRIAGE_CMD_REFLECT}")
   322→        print(colorize("    (Contradictions, recurring patterns, which direction to take, what to defer)", "dim"))
   323→    elif "organize" not in stages:
   324→        gaps = _unenriched_clusters(plan)
   325→        manual = manual_clusters_with_issues(plan)
   326→
   327→        if not manual:
   328→            print(colorize("  Next steps:", "yellow"))
   329→            print("    0. Defer contradictory issues: `desloppify plan skip <hash>`")
   330→            print(f"    1. Create clusters:  {TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_CREATE}")
   331→            print(f"    2. Add issues:     {TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_ADD}")
   332→            print(f"    3. Enrich clusters:  {TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_STEPS}")
   333→            print(f"    4. Record stage:     {TRIAGE_CMD_ORGANIZE}")
   334→        elif gaps:
   335→            print(colorize("  Enrich these clusters before recording organize:", "yellow"))
   336→            for name, missing in gaps:
   337→                print(colorize(f"    {name}: missing {', '.join(missing)}", "yellow"))
   338→            print(colorize(
   339→                f"    Fix: {TRIAGE_CMD_CLUSTER_ENRICH_COMPACT}",
   340→                "dim",
   341→            ))
   342→            print(colorize(f"    Then: {TRIAGE_CMD_ORGANIZE}", "dim"))
   343→        else:
   344→            print(colorize("  All clusters enriched! Record the organize stage:", "green"))
   345→            print(f"    {TRIAGE_CMD_ORGANIZE}")
   346→
   347→        if meta.get("strategy_summary"):
   348→            print()
   349→            print(colorize("  Or fast-track (if existing plan is still valid):", "dim"))
   350→            print(f"    {TRIAGE_CMD_CONFIRM_EXISTING}")
   351→    else:
   352→        print(colorize("  Ready to complete:", "green"))
   353→        print(f"    {TRIAGE_CMD_COMPLETE_VERBOSE}")
   354→        print(colorize('    (use --strategy "same" to keep existing strategy)', "dim"))
   355→
   356→
   357→def _print_prior_stage_reports(stages: dict) -> None:
   358→    """Print prior stage reports (observe/reflect) as context for current action."""
   359→    if "observe" in stages:
   360→        obs_report = stages["observe"].get("report", "")
   361→        if obs_report:
   362→            print(colorize("\n  Your observe analysis:", "dim"))
   363→            for line in obs_report.strip().splitlines()[:8]:
   364→                print(colorize(f"    {line}", "dim"))
   365→            if len(obs_report.strip().splitlines()) > 8:
   366→                print(colorize("    ...", "dim"))
   367→    if "reflect" in stages:
   368→        ref_report = stages["reflect"].get("report", "")
   369→        if ref_report:
   370→            print(colorize("\n  Your reflect strategy:", "dim"))
   371→            for line in ref_report.strip().splitlines()[:8]:
   372→                print(colorize(f"    {line}", "dim"))
   373→            if len(ref_report.strip().splitlines()) > 8:
   374→                print(colorize("    ...", "dim"))
   375→
   376→
   377→def _print_issues_by_dimension(open_issues: dict) -> None:
   378→    """Print issues grouped by dimension with suggestions to surface contradictions."""
   379→    by_dim: dict[str, list[tuple[str, dict]]] = defaultdict(list)
   380→    for fid, f in open_issues.items():
   381→        detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   382→        dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
   383→        by_dim[dim].append((fid, f))
   384→
   385→    print(colorize("\n  Review issues by dimension:", "cyan"))
   386→    print(colorize("  (Look for contradictions: issues in the same dimension that", "dim"))
   387→    print(colorize("  recommend opposite changes. These must be resolved before clustering.)", "dim"))
   388→    max_per_dim = 5
   389→    for dim in sorted(by_dim, key=lambda d: (-len(by_dim[d]), d)):
   390→        items = by_dim[dim]
   391→        print(colorize(f"\n    {dim} ({len(items)}):", "bold"))
   392→        for fid, f in items[:max_per_dim]:
   393→            summary = f.get("summary", "")
   394→            short = short_issue_id(fid)
   395→            detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   396→            suggestion = (detail.get("suggestion") or "")[:120]
   397→            print(f"      [{short}] {summary}")
   398→            if suggestion:
   399→                print(colorize(f"        \u2192 {suggestion}", "dim"))
   400→        if len(items) > max_per_dim:
   401→            print(colorize(f"      ... and {len(items) - max_per_dim} more", "dim"))
   402→    print(colorize("\n  Use hash in commands: desloppify plan skip <hash>  |  desloppify show <hash>", "dim"))
   403→
   404→
   405→def cmd_triage_dashboard(
   406→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   407→    *,
   408→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   409→) -> None:
   410→    """Default view: show issues, stage progress, and next command."""
   411→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   412→    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
   413→    state = runtime.state
   414→    plan = resolved_services.load_plan()
   415→    si = resolved_services.collect_triage_input(plan, state)
   416→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   417→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   418→
   419→    _print_dashboard_header(si, stages, meta, plan)
   420→    _print_action_guidance(stages, meta, si, plan)
   421→    _print_prior_stage_reports(stages)
   422→    _print_issues_by_dimension(si.open_issues)
   423→
   424→    # Show reflect dashboard when observe done, reflect not done
   425→    if "observe" in stages and "reflect" not in stages:
   426→        print_reflect_dashboard(si, plan, services=resolved_services)
   427→
   428→    # Show current cluster progress
   429→    print_progress(plan, si.open_issues)
   430→
   431→def show_plan_summary(plan: dict, state: dict) -> None:
   432→    """Print a compact plan rendering: clusters + queue order + coverage."""
   433→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   434→    active = {
   435→        n: c for n, c in clusters.items()
   436→        if c.get("issue_ids") and not c.get("auto")
   437→    }
   438→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
   439→
   440→    if active:
   441→        print(colorize(f"\n  Clusters ({len(active)}):", "bold"))
   442→        for name, cluster in active.items():
   443→            count = len(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   444→            steps = len(cluster.get("action_steps", []))
   445→            desc = (cluster.get("description") or "")[:60]
   446→            print(f"    {name}: {count} items, {steps} steps — {desc}")
   447→
   448→    queue_order = [
   449→        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   450→        if not fid.startswith("triage::") and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
   451→    ]
   452→    if queue_order:
   453→        show = min(15, len(queue_order))
   454→        print(colorize(f"\n  Queue order (first {show} of {len(queue_order)}):", "bold"))
   455→        for i, fid in enumerate(queue_order[:show]):
   456→            f = issues.get(fid, {})
   457→            summary = (f.get("summary") or fid)[:60]
   458→            detector = f.get("detector", "?")
   459→            cn = find_cluster_for(fid, active)
   460→            print(f"    {i+1}. [{detector}] {summary}{f' ({cn})' if cn else ''}")
   461→
   462→    organized, total, _ = triage_coverage(
   463→        plan, open_review_ids=open_review_ids_from_state(state),
   464→    )
   465→    pct = int(organized / total * 100) if total else 0
   466→    print(colorize(f"\n  Coverage: {organized}/{total} in clusters ({pct}%)", "bold"))
   467→
   468→__all__ = [
   469→    "cmd_triage_dashboard",
   470→    "print_organize_result",
   471→    "print_progress",
   472→    "print_reflect_dashboard",
   473→    "print_reflect_result",
   474→    "print_stage_progress",
   475→    "show_plan_summary",
   476→]
   477→
```

```
"""Canonical public source-discovery and file-cache API."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

from . import source as source_discovery_mod

DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS = source_discovery_mod.DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS


def _normalize_patterns(patterns: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    normalized: list[str] = []
    seen: set[str] = set()
    for raw in patterns:
        text = str(raw).strip()
        if not text or text in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(text)
        normalized.append(text)
    return normalized


def _normalize_extensions(extensions: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    normalized: list[str] = []
    seen: set[str] = set()
    for raw in extensions:
        text = str(raw).strip()
        if not text:
            continue
```

```
1→"""Tests for plan reconciliation — supersede, prune, cluster desync fix."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.operations_cluster import add_to_cluster, create_cluster
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.reconcile import reconcile_plan_after_scan
     7→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan, ensure_plan_defaults
     8→
     9→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    10→# Helpers
    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→
    13→def _plan_with_queue(*ids: str) -> dict:
    14→    plan = empty_plan()
    15→    plan["queue_order"] = list(ids)
    16→    return plan
    17→
    18→
    19→def _state_with_issues(*ids: str, status: str = "open") -> dict:
    20→    issues = {}
    21→    for fid in ids:
    22→        issues[fid] = {
    23→            "id": fid,
    24→            "status": status,
    25→            "detector": "test",
    26→            "file": "test.py",
    27→            "tier": 1,
    28→            "confidence": "high",
    29→            "summary": f"Issue {fid}",
    30→        }
    31→    return {"issues": issues, "scan_count": 5}
    32→
    33→
    34→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    35→# _supersede_id clears override cluster ref
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→
    38→def test_supersede_clears_override_cluster_ref():
    39→    """When a issue is superseded, its override cluster ref should be cleared."""
    40→    plan = _plan_with_queue("a", "b")
    41→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    42→
    43→    # Create a cluster and add issue "a" to it
    44→    create_cluster(plan, "my-cluster")
    45→    add_to_cluster(plan, "my-cluster", ["a"])
    46→
    47→    # Verify override has cluster ref
    48→    assert plan["overrides"]["a"]["cluster"] == "my-cluster"
    49→
    50→    # State where "a" is gone (not present = not alive)
    51→    state = _state_with_issues("b")
    52→
    53→    result = reconcile_plan_after_scan(plan, state)
    54→    assert "a" in result.superseded
    55→
    56→    # Override cluster ref should be cleared
    57→    override = plan["overrides"].get("a")
    58→    assert override is not None
    59→    assert override.get("cluster") is None
    60→
    61→
    62→def test_supersede_preserves_note_in_override():
    63→    """Superseding should preserve the note in the override (for history)."""
    64→    plan = _plan_with_queue("a")
    65→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    66→
    67→    # Add an override with a note
    68→    plan["overrides"]["a"] = {
    69→        "issue_id": "a",
    70→        "note": "important context",
    71→        "cluster": None,
    72→        "created_at": "2025-01-01T00:00:00+00:00",
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    state = _state_with_issues()  # "a" is gone
    76→    reconcile_plan_after_scan(plan, state)
    77→
    78→    # Superseded entry should have the note
    79→    assert plan["superseded"]["a"]["note"] == "important context"
    80→
    81→
    82→def test_supersede_removes_from_cluster_issue_ids():
    83→    """Superseded issue should be removed from cluster issue_ids."""
    84→    plan = _plan_with_queue("a", "b")
    85→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    86→
    87→    create_cluster(plan, "my-cluster")
    88→    add_to_cluster(plan, "my-cluster", ["a", "b"])
    89→
    90→    # "a" disappears
    91→    state = _state_with_issues("b")
    92→    reconcile_plan_after_scan(plan, state)
    93→
    94→    assert "a" not in plan["clusters"]["my-cluster"]["issue_ids"]
    95→    assert "b" in plan["clusters"]["my-cluster"]["issue_ids"]
    96→
    97→
    98→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    99→# Reconcile logs execution
   100→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```
